Genesis 2
2 Thus the heavens and the earth were completed in all their vast array.
(A)
2 By the seventh day God had finished the work he had been doing; so on the
(B)
seventh day he rested from all his work. 3 Then God blessed the seventh
(C)
day and made it holy, because on it he rested from all the work of
(D) (E)
creating that he had done.
(F)
Adam and Eve
4 This is the account of the heavens and the earth when they were created, when
(G) (H)
the LORD God made the earth and the heavens.
5 Now no shrub had yet appeared on the earth and no plant had yet sprung up, for
[a] (I)
the LORD God had not sent rain on the earth
(J)
and there was no one to work the ground,
6 but streams came up from the earth and watered the whole surface of the ground. 7
[b]
Then the LORD God formed
(K)
a man
[c](L)
from the dust
(M)
of the ground
(N)
and breathed
into his nostrils the breath of life, and the man became a living being.
(O) (P) (Q)
8 Now the LORD God had planted a garden in the east, in Eden;
(R)
and there he put the
man he had formed. 9 The LORD God made all kinds of trees grow out of the
ground—trees that were pleasing to the eye and good for food. In the middle of the
(S)
garden were the tree of life and the tree of the knowledge of good and evil.
(T) (U)

10 A river watering the garden flowed from Eden; from there it was separated into
(V) (W)
four headwaters. 11 The name of the first is the Pishon; it winds through the entire land
of Havilah, where there is gold. 12 (The gold of that land is good; aromatic resin
(X) [d](Y)
and onyx are also there.) 13 The name of the second river is the Gihon; it winds through
the entire land of Cush. 14 The name of the third river is the Tigris; it runs along the
[e] (Z)
east side of Ashur. And the fourth river is the Euphrates.
(AA)
15 The LORD God took the man and put him in the Garden of Eden
(AB)
to work it and take
care of it. 16 And the LORD God commanded the man, “You are free to eat from any tree
in the garden; 17 but you must not eat from the tree of the knowledge of good and
(AC)
evil, for when you eat from it you will certainly die.”
(AD) (AE)
18 The LORD God said, “It is not good for the man to be alone. I will make a helper
suitable for him.”
(AF)
19 Now the LORD God had formed out of the ground all the wild animals
(AG)
and all the
birds in the sky. He brought them to the man to see what he would name them; and
(AH)
whatever the man called each living creature, that was its name. 20 So the man
(AI) (AJ)
gave names to all the livestock, the birds in the sky and all the wild animals.
But for Adam
[f]
no suitable helper
(AK)
was found. 21 So the LORD God caused the man to
fall into a deep sleep; and while he was sleeping, he took one of the man’s ribs and
(AL) [g]
then closed up the place with flesh. 22 Then the LORD God made a woman from the
rib he had taken out of the man, and he brought her to the man.
[h](AM)
23 The man said,
“This is now bone of my bones
and flesh of my flesh;
(AN)
she shall be called ‘woman,’
(AO)
for she was taken out of man. ”
(AP)

24 That is why a man leaves his father and mother and is united to his wife, and they
(AQ)
become one flesh.
(AR)
25 Adam and his wife were both naked, and they felt no shame.
(AS)

Genesis 3
The Fall
3 Now the serpent
(A)
was more crafty than any of the wild animals the LORD God had
made. He said to the woman, “Did God really say, ‘You must not eat from any tree in the
garden’? ”
(B)
2 The woman said to the serpent, “We may eat fruit from the trees in the garden, 3 but
(C)
God did say, ‘You must not eat fruit from the tree that is in the middle of the garden, and
you must not touch it, or you will die.’”
(D)
4 “You will not certainly die,” the serpent said to the woman. 5 “For God knows that
(E)
when you eat from it your eyes will be opened, and you will be like God, knowing good
(F)
and evil.”
6 When the woman saw that the fruit of the tree was good for food and pleasing to the
eye, and also desirable for gaining wisdom, she took some and ate it. She also gave
(G)
some to her husband, who was with her, and he ate it. 7 Then the eyes of both of
(H) (I)

them were opened, and they realized they were naked; so they sewed fig leaves
(J)
together and made coverings for themselves.
(K)
8 Then the man and his wife heard the sound of the LORD God as he was walking
(L)
in the
garden in the cool of the day, and they hid
(M)
from the LORD God among the trees of the
garden. 9 But the LORD God called to the man, “Where are you?”
(N)
10 He answered, “I heard you in the garden, and I was afraid because I was naked;
(O) (P)
so I hid.”
11 And he said, “Who told you that you were naked? Have you eaten from the tree that
(Q)
I commanded you not to eat from? ”
(R)
12 The man said, “The woman you put here with me —she gave me some fruit from the
(S)
tree, and I ate it.”
13 Then the LORD God said to the woman, “What is this you have done?”
The woman said, “The serpent deceived me, and I ate.”
(T)
14 So the LORD God said to the serpent, “Because you have done this,
“Cursed are you above all livestock
(U)
and all wild animals!
You will crawl on your belly
and you will eat dust
(V)
all the days of your life.

between you and the woman,

[a] (A) (B)

[c]

(E)

(F)

(H)

(K)

(O)
“I don’t know, ” he replied. “Am I my brother’s keeper?”

(V)

” Then the LORD put a mark on Cain so that no one who found him

and lived in the land of

then Lamech seventy-seven times. ”

” 30 After Noah was born, Lamech lived 595 years and had other

8 But God remembered Noah and all the wild animals and the livestock that were with
(A)
him in the ark, and he sent a wind over the earth, and the waters receded. 2 Now the
(B)

(C) (D)

(E)

(F) (G) (H)

the tops of the mountains became visible.
6 After forty days Noah opened a window he had made in the ark 7 and sent out a
(I)

(J) (K)
Then he sent out a dove to see if the water had receded from the surface of the
(L)
ground. 9 But the dove could find nowhere to perch because there was water over all the
surface of the earth; so it returned to Noah in the ark. He reached out his hand and took
the dove and brought it back to himself in the ark. 10 He waited seven more days and
again sent out the dove from the ark. 11 When the dove returned to him in the evening,
there in its beak was a freshly plucked olive leaf! Then Noah knew that the water had
receded from the earth. 12 He waited seven more days and sent the dove out again,
(M)
but this time it did not return to him.
13 By the first day of the first month of Noah’s six hundred and first year, the water
(N)
had dried up from the earth. Noah then removed the covering from the ark and saw that

the surface of the ground was dry. 14 By the twenty-seventh day of the second month
(O)
the earth was completely dry.

and their wives. 17 Bring out every kind of living creature that is with you—the birds,
(P)

(R)

became the father of Peleg, Eber lived 430 years and had other sons and daughters.
18 When Peleg had lived 30 years, he became the father of Reu. 19 And after he
(Z)
became the father of Reu, Peleg lived 209 years and had other sons and daughters.
20 When Reu had lived 32 years, he became the father of Serug. 21 And after he
(AA)
became the father of Serug, Reu lived 207 years and had other sons and daughters.
22 When Serug had lived 30 years, he became the father of Nahor. 23 And after he
(AB)
became the father of Nahor, Serug lived 200 years and had other sons and daughters.

24 When Nahor had lived 29 years, he became the father of Terah. 25 And after he
(AC)
became the father of Terah, Nahor lived 119 years and had other sons and daughters.
26 After Terah had lived 70 years, he became the father of Abram, Nahor and
(AD) (AE)
Haran.
(AF)
Abram’s Family
27 This is the account of Terah’s family line.
(AG)
Terah became the father of Abram, Nahor and Haran. And Haran became the father
(AH)
of Lot. 28 While his father Terah was still alive, Haran died in Ur of the Chaldeans,
(AI) (AJ)
in the land of his birth. 29 Abram and Nahor both married. The name of Abram’s wife
(AK)
was Sarai, and the name of Nahor’s wife was Milkah; she was the daughter of
(AL) (AM)
Haran, the father of both Milkah and Iskah. 30 Now Sarai was childless because she
was not able to conceive.
(AN)
31 Terah took his son Abram, his grandson Lot son of Haran, and his
(AO)
daughter-in-law Sarai, the wife of his son Abram, and together they set out from Ur of
(AP)
the Chaldeans to go to Canaan. But when they came to Harran, they settled
(AQ) (AR) (AS)
there.
32 Terah lived 205 years, and he died in Harran.

Abram and Lot Separate

13 So Abram went up from Egypt to the Negev, with his wife and everything he had,
(A) (B)
and Lot went with him. 2 Abram had become very wealthy in livestock and in silver
(C) (D) (E)

of the Jordan and set out toward the east. The two men parted company: 12 Abram
lived in the land of Canaan, while Lot lived among the cities of the plain and

(AD) (AE)

Abram Rescues Lot

14 At the time when Amraphel was king of Shinar, Arioch king of Ellasar,
[a](A)
Kedorlaomer king of Elam and Tidal king of Goyim, 2 these kings went to war
(B) (C)

(AD)
he was living in Sodom.
13 A man who had escaped came and reported this to Abram the Hebrew. Now

Abram was living near the great trees of Mamre the Amorite, a brother of Eshkol
(AF) [b] (AG)
and Aner, all of whom were allied with Abram. 14 When Abram heard that his relative

had been taken captive, he called out the 318 trained men born in his household

(AQ)
Valley).

(AW) (AX)

(AY)

20

(BA)
who delivered your enemies into your hand.”
Then Abram gave him a tenth of everything.

(BC) (BD)
yourself.”
22 But Abram said to the king of Sodom, “With raised hand I have sworn an oath to
(BE) (BF)
the LORD, God Most High,
(BG)
Creator of heaven and earth,
(BH)
23 that I will accept nothing
belonging to you, not even a thread or the strap of a sandal, so that you will never be

The LORD’s Covenant With Abram
15 After this, the word of the LORD came to Abram
(A)
in a vision:
(B)
“Do not be afraid, Abram.
(C)
I am your shield,
[a](D)
your very great reward. ”
[b](E)

(F)
what can you give me since I remain childless
(G)
and the one who will inherit my estate is Eliezer of Damascus? ” 3 And Abram said,
[c] (H)
“You have given me no children; so a servant in my household will be my heir.”
(I) (J)

is your own flesh and blood will be your heir. ” 5 He took him outside and said, “Look
(K)

(L)
“So shall your offspring be.”
[d] (M)

6 Abram believed the LORD, and he credited it to him as righteousness.
(N)

(O)

(P)
to

8 But Abram said, “Sovereign LORD,
(R)

that I will gain possession of

9 So the LORD said to him, “Bring me a heifer,
(U)

(V)

10 Abram brought all these to him, cut them in two and arranged the halves opposite
each other; the birds, however, he did not cut in half. 11 Then birds of prey came

down on the carcasses, but Abram drove them away.

(AA)

(AB)
they will be enslaved and mistreated there. 14 But I will punish the nation they serve
(AC)
as slaves, and afterward they will come out with great possessions. 15 You,
(AD) (AE)
however, will go to your ancestors in peace and be buried at a good old age. 16 In
(AF) (AG)
the fourth generation your descendants will come back here, for the sin of the
(AH) (AI)

(AJ)

(A) (B)
slave
(C)

Genesis 18
The Three Visitors

18 The LORD appeared to Abraham
(A)
near the great trees of Mamre
(B)
while he was
sitting at the entrance to his tent in the heat of the day. 2 Abraham looked up and
(C) (D)
saw three men standing nearby. When he saw them, he hurried from the entrance of
(E)
his tent to meet them and bowed low to the ground.
(F)

(G) [a] (H)

who hurried to prepare it. 8 He then brought some curds and milk and the calf that
(M) (N)
had been prepared, and set these before them. While they ate, he stood near them

under a tree.

(P)

10 Then one of them said, “I will surely return to you about this time next year, and
(R)
Sarah your wife will have a son.”
(S)
Now Sarah was listening at the entrance to the tent, which was behind him. 11 Abraham
and Sarah were already very old, and Sarah was past the age of childbearing. 12 So
(T) (U)
Sarah laughed to herself as she thought, “After I am worn out and my lord is old, will
(V) (W)
I now have this pleasure?”

13 Then the LORD said to Abraham, “Why did Sarah laugh and say, ‘Will I really have a
child, now that I am old?’
(X)
14 Is anything too hard for the LORD?
(Y)
I will return to you at
the appointed time next year, and Sarah will have a son.”
(Z) (AA)
15 Sarah was afraid, so she lied and said, “I did not laugh.”
But he said, “Yes, you did laugh.”
Abraham Pleads for Sodom
16 When the men got up to leave, they looked down toward Sodom, and Abraham
(AB)
walked along with them to see them on their way. 17 Then the LORD said, “Shall I hide
from Abraham what I am about to do? 18 Abraham will surely become a great and
(AC) (AD)
powerful nation, and all nations on earth will be blessed through him. 19 For I have
(AE) [c]
chosen him , so that he will direct his children and his household after him to keep
(AF) (AG)
the way of the LORD(AH) by doing what is right and just,
(AI)
so that the LORD will bring about
for Abraham what he has promised him.”
(AJ)
20 Then the LORD said, “The outcry against Sodom
(AK)
and Gomorrah is so great
(AL)
and
their sin so grievous 21 that I will go down and see if what they have done is as
(AM) (AN)
bad as the outcry that has reached me. If not, I will know.”
22 The men turned away and went toward Sodom, but Abraham remained
(AO) (AP)
standing before the LORD.
[d](AQ)
23 Then Abraham approached him and said: “Will you
sweep away the righteous with the wicked? 24 What if there are fifty righteous people
(AR)
in the city? Will you really sweep it away and not spare the place for the sake of the
[e]
fifty righteous people in it? 25 Far be it from you to do such a thing —to kill the
(AS) (AT)
righteous with the wicked, treating the righteous and the wicked alike. Far be it
(AU) (AV)
from you! Will not the Judge of all the earth do right?”
(AW) (AX)

26 The LORD said, “If I find fifty righteous people in the city of Sodom, I will spare the
whole place for their sake. ”
(AY)
27 Then Abraham spoke up again: “Now that I have been so bold as to speak to the
Lord, though I am nothing but dust and ashes, 28 what if the number of the righteous
(AZ)
is five less than fifty? Will you destroy the whole city for lack of five people?”
“If I find forty-five there,” he said, “I will not destroy it.”
29 Once again he spoke to him, “What if only forty are found there?”
He said, “For the sake of forty, I will not do it.”
30 Then he said, “May the Lord not be angry, but let me speak. What if only thirty can
(BA)
be found there?”
He answered, “I will not do it if I find thirty there.”
31 Abraham said, “Now that I have been so bold as to speak to the Lord, what if only
twenty can be found there?”
He said, “For the sake of twenty, I will not destroy it.”
32 Then he said, “May the Lord not be angry, but let me speak just once more. What if
(BB)
only ten can be found there?”
He answered, “For the sake of ten, I will not destroy it.”
(BC)
33 When the LORD had finished speaking
(BD)
with Abraham, he left,
(BE)
and Abraham
returned home.
(BF)

Genesis 19
Sodom and Gomorrah Destroyed
19 The two angels arrived at Sodom in the evening, and Lot was sitting in the
(A) (B) (C)
gateway of the city. When he saw them, he got up to meet them and bowed down with
(D)
his face to the ground. 2 “My lords,” he said, “please turn aside to your servant’s
(E)
house. You can wash your feet and spend the night and then go on your way early in
(F)
the morning.”
“No,” they answered, “we will spend the night in the square.”
(G)
3 But he insisted so strongly that they did go with him and entered his house. He
(H) (I)
prepared a meal for them, baking bread without yeast, and they ate. 4 Before they
(J) (K)
had gone to bed, all the men from every part of the city of Sodom —both young and
(L)
old—surrounded the house. 5 They called to Lot, “Where are the men who came to you

(M)

6 Lot went outside to meet them and shut the door behind him 7 and said, “No, my
(N)

(O)

(P)

(Q)

10 But the men inside reached out and pulled Lot back into the house and shut the
(R)
door. 11 Then they struck the men who were at the door of the house, young and old,

(S)
12 The two men said to Lot, “Do you have anyone else here—sons-in-law, sons or

(T)
because we
(U)
are going to destroy this place. The outcry to the LORD against its people
is so great that he has sent us to destroy it.”
(V) (W)
14 So Lot went out and spoke to his sons-in-law, who were pledged to marry his
[a]
daughters. He said, “Hurry and get out of this place, because the LORD is about to

(X) (Y)
15 With the coming of dawn, the angels urged Lot, saying, “Hurry! Take your wife and
your two daughters who are here, or you will be swept away when the city is
(Z)
punished. ”
(AA)

(AB)
and led them safely out of the city, for the LORD was merciful to

(AC)

(AD) (AE) (AF)
mountains or you will be swept away!”
(AG)

18 But Lot said to them, “No, my lords, please! 19 Your servant has found favor in
[b] [c]
your eyes, and you have shown great kindness to me in sparing my life. But I
[d] (AH) [e] (AI)

Then my life will be spared.”
21 He said to him, “Very well, I will grant this request too; I will not overthrow the town
(AK)
you speak of. 22 But flee there quickly, because I cannot do anything until you reach it.”

Genesis 20
Abraham and Abimelek(A)
20 Now Abraham moved on from there into the region of the Negev and lived
(B) (C)
between Kadesh and Shur. For a while he stayed in Gerar, 2 and there Abraham
(D) (E) (F) (G)
said of his wife Sarah, “She is my sister. ” Then Abimelek king of Gerar sent for Sarah
(H) (I)
and took her.
(J)

(K) (L)
as dead because of the woman you have taken; she is a married woman.”
(M) (N)

brother’? I have done this with a clear conscience and clean hands. ”

(S) (T)

will live. But if you do not return her, you may be sure that you and all who belong to you

(Y)

Genesis 21
The Birth of Isaac
21 Now the LORD was gracious to Sarah
(A)
as he had said, and the LORD did for Sarah
what he had promised. 2 Sarah became pregnant and bore a son to Abraham in his
(B) (C)
old age, at the very time God had promised him. 3 Abraham gave the name Isaac
(D) (E) [a](F)
to the son Sarah bore him. 4 When his son Isaac was eight days old, Abraham
circumcised him, as God commanded him. 5 Abraham was a hundred years old
(G) (H)
when his son Isaac was born to him.
6 Sarah said, “God has brought me laughter, and everyone who hears about this will
(I)
laugh with me.” 7 And she added, “Who would have said to Abraham that Sarah would

(J)
Hagar and Ishmael Sent Away
8 The child grew and was weaned, and on the day Isaac was weaned Abraham held a
(K)
great feast. 9 But Sarah saw that the son whom Hagar the Egyptian had borne to

(L) (M) (N)
and her son, for that woman’s son will never share in the inheritance with my son

(O)

(P)
said to him, “Do not be so distressed about the boy and your slave woman. Listen to

reckoned. 13 I will make the son of the slave into a nation also, because he is your
(Q) (R)
offspring.”

(S)
on her way and wandered in the Desert of Beersheba.

(V) (W) (X)
and said to her, “What is the matter, Hagar? Do not be afraid; God has heard the boy

called Rebekah and asked her, “Will you go with this man?”
“I will go,” she said.
(DO)
59 So they sent their sister Rebekah on her way, along with her nurse and
(DP) (DQ)
Abraham’s servant and his men. 60 And they blessed Rebekah and said to her,
(DR)
“Our sister, may you increase
to thousands upon thousands;
(DS)
may your offspring possess
the cities of their enemies.”
(DT)
61 Then Rebekah and her attendants got ready and mounted the camels and went
(DU)
back with the man. So the servant took Rebekah and left.
62 Now Isaac had come from Beer Lahai Roi, for he was living in the Negev. 63 He
(DV) (DW)
went out to the field one evening to meditate, and as he looked up, he saw
[f](DX) (DY)
camels approaching. 64 Rebekah also looked up and saw Isaac. She got down from her
camel 65 and asked the servant, “Who is that man in the field coming to meet us?”
(DZ)
“He is my master,” the servant answered. So she took her veil and covered herself.
(EA)

66 Then the servant told Isaac all he had done. 67 Isaac brought her into the tent of
(EB)
his mother Sarah, and he married Rebekah. So she became his wife, and he loved
(EC) (ED)
her; and Isaac was comforted after his mother’s death.
(EE) (EF)

Genesis 25
The Death of Abraham(A)
25 Abraham had taken another wife, whose name was Keturah. 2 She bore him
Zimran,  Jokshan, Medan, Midian,  Ishbak and Shuah.  3 Jokshan was the father of
| (B) |     | (C) |     | (D) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
Sheba  and Dedan;  the descendants of Dedan were the Ashurites, the Letushites and
| (E) | (F) |     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
the Leummites. 4 The sons of Midian were Ephah, (G)  Epher, Hanok, Abida and Eldaah. All
these were descendants of Keturah.
5 Abraham left everything he owned to Isaac.  6 But while he was still living, he gave
(H)
gifts to the sons of his concubines  and sent them away from his son Isaac  to the
(I) (J)
| land of the east. | (K)  |     |     |     |
| ----------------- | ---- | --- | --- | --- |
7 Abraham lived a hundred and seventy-five years.  8 Then Abraham breathed his last
(L)
and died at a good old age,  an old man and full of years; and he was gathered to his
(M)
people. (N)  9 His sons Isaac and Ishmael buried him (O)  in the cave of Machpelah (P)  near
Mamre,  in the field of Ephron  son of Zohar the Hittite,  10 the field Abraham had
| (Q) |     | (R) |     | (S) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
bought from the Hittites.  There Abraham was buried with his wife Sarah. 11 After
[a](T)
Abraham’s death, God blessed his son Isaac,  who then lived near Beer Lahai Roi.
(U) (V)
Ishmael’s Sons(W)
12 This is the account  of the family line of Abraham’s son Ishmael, whom Sarah’s
(X)
| slave, Hagar | (Y)  the Egyptian, bore to Abraham. |     | (Z)  |     |
| ------------ | ----------------------------------- | --- | ---- | --- |

13 These are the names of the sons of Ishmael, listed in the order of their birth:
Nebaioth the firstborn of Ishmael, Kedar, Adbeel, Mibsam, 14 Mishma, Dumah,
(AA) (AB) (AC)
Massa, 15 Hadad, Tema, Jetur, Naphish and Kedemah. 16 These were the sons of
(AD) (AE)
Ishmael, and these are the names of the twelve tribal rulers according to their
(AF)
settlements and camps. 17 Ishmael lived a hundred and thirty-seven years. He
(AG)
breathed his last and died, and he was gathered to his people. 18 His descendants
(AH) (AI)
settled in the area from Havilah to Shur, near the eastern border of Egypt, as you go
(AJ)
toward Ashur. And they lived in hostility toward all the tribes related to them.
[b] (AK)
Jacob and Esau
19 This is the account of the family line of Abraham’s son Isaac.
(AL)
Abraham became the father of Isaac, 20 and Isaac was forty years old when he
(AM)
married Rebekah daughter of Bethuel the Aramean from Paddan Aram and
(AN) (AO) [c](AP)
sister of Laban the Aramean.
(AQ) (AR)
21 Isaac prayed to the LORD on behalf of his wife, because she was childless.
(AS)
The
LORD answered his prayer,
(AT)
and his wife Rebekah became pregnant. 22 The babies
jostled each other within her, and she said, “Why is this happening to me?” So she went
to inquire of the LORD.
(AU)
23 The LORD said to her,
“Two nations are in your womb,
(AV)
and two peoples from within you will be separated;
one people will be stronger than the other,
and the older will serve the younger. ”
(AW)
24 When the time came for her to give birth, there were twin boys in her womb. 25
(AX) (AY)
The first to come out was red, and his whole body was like a hairy garment; so
(AZ) (BA)

they named him Esau. 26 After this, his brother came out, with his hand grasping
[d](BB) (BC)
Esau’s heel; so he was named Jacob. Isaac was sixty years old when Rebekah
(BD) [e](BE) (BF)
gave birth to them.
27 The boys grew up, and Esau became a skillful hunter, a man of the open
(BG)
country, while Jacob was content to stay at home among the tents. 28 Isaac, who
(BH)
had a taste for wild game, loved Esau, but Rebekah loved Jacob.
(BI) (BJ)
29 Once when Jacob was cooking some stew, Esau came in from the open
(BK)
country, famished. 30 He said to Jacob, “Quick, let me have some of that red stew!
(BL) (BM)
I’m famished!” (That is why he was also called Edom. )
[f] (BN)
31 Jacob replied, “First sell me your birthright. ”
(BO)
32 “Look, I am about to die,” Esau said. “What good is the birthright to me?”
33 But Jacob said, “Swear to me first.” So he swore an oath to him, selling his
(BP)
birthright to Jacob.
(BQ)
34 Then Jacob gave Esau some bread and some lentil stew. He ate and drank, and
(BR)
then got up and left.
So Esau despised his birthright.

a. Genesis 25:10 Or the descendants of Heth
b. Genesis 25:18 Or lived to the east of
c. Genesis 25:20 That is, Northwest Mesopotamia
d. Genesis 25:25 Esau may mean hairy.
e. Genesis 25:26 Jacob means he grasps the heel, a Hebrew idiom for he deceives.

f. Genesis 25:30 Edom means red.

Genesis 26
Isaac and Abimelek(A)
26 Now there was a famine in the land —besides the previous famine in Abraham’s
(B)
time—and Isaac went to Abimelek king of the Philistines
(C)
in Gerar.
(D)
2 The LORD
appeared to Isaac and said, “Do not go down to Egypt; live in the land where I tell you
(E) (F)
to live. 3 Stay in this land for a while, and I will be with you and will bless you. For
(G) (H) (I) (J)
to you and your descendants I will give all these lands and will confirm the oath I
(K)
swore to your father Abraham. 4 I will make your descendants as numerous as the
(L) (M)

stars in the sky and will give them all these lands, and through your offspring all
(N) (O) [a]
nations on earth will be blessed, 5 because Abraham obeyed me and did
[b](P) (Q)
everything I required of him, keeping my commands, my decrees and my
(R)
instructions. ” 6 So Isaac stayed in Gerar.
(S) (T)
7 When the men of that place asked him about his wife, he said, “She is my sister, ”
(U)
because he was afraid to say, “She is my wife.” He thought, “The men of this place might
kill me on account of Rebekah, because she is beautiful.”
8 When Isaac had been there a long time, Abimelek king of the Philistines looked
(V)
down from a window and saw Isaac caressing his wife Rebekah. 9 So Abimelek
summoned Isaac and said, “She is really your wife! Why did you say, ‘She is my
sister’? ”
(W)
Isaac answered him, “Because I thought I might lose my life on account of her.”
10 Then Abimelek said, “What is this you have done to us? One of the men might well
(X)
have slept with your wife, and you would have brought guilt upon us.”
11 So Abimelek gave orders to all the people: “Anyone who harms this man or his wife
(Y)
shall surely be put to death.”
(Z)
12 Isaac planted crops in that land and the same year reaped a hundredfold, because
(AA)
the LORD blessed him.
(AB)
13 The man became rich, and his wealth continued to grow
until he became very wealthy. 14 He had so many flocks and herds and servants
(AC) (AD)
that the Philistines envied him. 15 So all the wells that his father’s servants had
(AE) (AF)
dug in the time of his father Abraham, the Philistines stopped up, filling them with
(AG)
earth.
16 Then Abimelek said to Isaac, “Move away from us; you have become too powerful
(AH)
for us. ”
(AI)

17 So Isaac moved away from there and encamped in the Valley of Gerar, where he
(AJ)
settled. 18 Isaac reopened the wells that had been dug in the time of his father
(AK)
Abraham, which the Philistines had stopped up after Abraham died, and he gave them
the same names his father had given them.
19 Isaac’s servants dug in the valley and discovered a well of fresh water there. 20 But
the herders of Gerar quarreled with those of Isaac and said, “The water is ours!”
(AL) (AM)
So he named the well Esek, because they disputed with him. 21 Then they dug another
[c]
well, but they quarreled over that one also; so he named it Sitnah. 22 He moved on
(AN) [d]
from there and dug another well, and no one quarreled over it. He named it
Rehoboth,
[e](AO)
saying, “Now the LORD has given us room
(AP)
and we will flourish
(AQ)
in the
land.”
23 From there he went up to Beersheba.
(AR)
24 That night the LORD appeared to him and
said, “I am the God of your father Abraham. Do not be afraid, for I am with you; I
(AS) (AT) (AU)
will bless you and will increase the number of your descendants for the sake of my
(AV)
servant Abraham.”
(AW)
25 Isaac built an altar
(AX)
there and called on the name of the LORD.
(AY)
There he pitched
his tent, and there his servants dug a well.
(AZ)
26 Meanwhile, Abimelek had come to him from Gerar, with Ahuzzath his personal
adviser and Phicol the commander of his forces. 27 Isaac asked them, “Why have
(BA)
you come to me, since you were hostile to me and sent me away? ”
(BB)
28 They answered, “We saw clearly that the LORD was with you;
(BC)
so we said, ‘There
ought to be a sworn agreement between us’—between us and you. Let us make a
treaty with you 29 that you will do us no harm, just as we did not harm you but
(BD) (BE)
always treated you well and sent you away peacefully. And now you are blessed by the
LORD.”
(BF)

30 Isaac then made a feast for them, and they ate and drank. 31 Early the next
(BG)
morning the men swore an oath to each other. Then Isaac sent them on their way,
(BH)
and they went away peacefully.
32 That day Isaac’s servants came and told him about the well they had dug. They
(BI)
said, “We’ve found water!” 33 He called it Shibah, and to this day the name of the town
[f]
has been Beersheba.
[g](BJ)
Jacob Takes Esau’s Blessing
34 When Esau was forty years old, he married Judith daughter of Beeri the Hittite,
(BK)
and also Basemath daughter of Elon the Hittite. 35 They were a source of grief to
(BL)
Isaac and Rebekah.
(BM)

Genesis 27
27 When Isaac was old and his eyes were so weak that he could no longer see, he
(A)
called for Esau his older son and said to him, “My son.”
(B)
“Here I am,” he answered.
2 Isaac said, “I am now an old man and don’t know the day of my death. 3 Now then,
(C)
get your equipment—your quiver and bow—and go out to the open country to hunt
(D)
some wild game for me. 4 Prepare me the kind of tasty food I like and bring it to me to
(E)
eat, so that I may give you my blessing before I die.”
(F) (G)
5 Now Rebekah was listening as Isaac spoke to his son Esau. When Esau left for the
open country to hunt game and bring it back, 6 Rebekah said to her son Jacob,
(H) (I)
“Look, I overheard your father say to your brother Esau, 7 ‘Bring me some game and
prepare me some tasty food to eat, so that I may give you my blessing in the presence
of the LORD before I die.’
(J)
8 Now, my son, listen carefully and do what I tell you:
(K)
9 Go
out to the flock and bring me two choice young goats, so I can prepare some tasty
(L)
food for your father, just the way he likes it. 10 Then take it to your father to eat, so
(M)
that he may give you his blessing before he dies.”
(N)
11 Jacob said to Rebekah his mother, “But my brother Esau is a hairy man while I have
(O)
smooth skin. 12 What if my father touches me? I would appear to be tricking him and
(P)
would bring down a curse on myself rather than a blessing.”
(Q)
13 His mother said to him, “My son, let the curse fall on me. Just do what I say; go
(R) (S)
and get them for me.”

14 So he went and got them and brought them to his mother, and she prepared some
tasty food, just the way his father liked it. 15 Then Rebekah took the best clothes of
(T) (U)
Esau her older son, which she had in the house, and put them on her younger son
(V)
Jacob. 16 She also covered his hands and the smooth part of his neck with the
goatskins. 17 Then she handed to her son Jacob the tasty food and the bread she
(W)
had made.
18 He went to his father and said, “My father.”
“Yes, my son,” he answered. “Who is it?”
(X)
19 Jacob said to his father, “I am Esau your firstborn. I have done as you told me.
(Y)
Please sit up and eat some of my game, so that you may give me your blessing.”
(Z) (AA)
20 Isaac asked his son, “How did you find it so quickly, my son?”
“The LORD your God gave me success,
(AB)
” he replied.
21 Then Isaac said to Jacob, “Come near so I can touch you, my son, to know
(AC)
whether you really are my son Esau or not.”
22 Jacob went close to his father Isaac, who touched him and said, “The voice is
(AD) (AE)
the voice of Jacob, but the hands are the hands of Esau.” 23 He did not recognize him,
for his hands were hairy like those of his brother Esau; so he proceeded to bless him.
(AF)
24 “Are you really my son Esau?” he asked.
“I am,” he replied.
25 Then he said, “My son, bring me some of your game to eat, so that I may give you my
blessing.”
(AG)

Jacob brought it to him and he ate; and he brought some wine and he drank. 26 Then
his father Isaac said to him, “Come here, my son, and kiss me.”
27 So he went to him and kissed  him . When Isaac caught the smell of his
|     | (AH) (AI) |     |
| --- | --------- | --- |
clothes, (AJ)  he blessed him and said,
“Ah, the smell of my son
is like the smell of a field

that the LORD has blessed.
(AK)

28
| May God give you heaven’s dew | (AL)           |       |
| ----------------------------- | -------------- | ----- |
| and earth’s richness          | —              |       |

29
May nations serve you
and peoples bow down to you. (AP)

30 After Isaac finished blessing him, and Jacob had scarcely left his father’s presence,
his brother Esau came in from hunting. 31 He too prepared some tasty food and
brought it to his father. Then he said to him, “My father, please sit up and eat some of
| my game, so that you may give me your blessing.” |     | (AS)  |
| ------------------------------------------------ | --- | ----- |
| 32 His father Isaac asked him, “Who are you?”    |     | (AT)  |

“I am your son,” he answered, “your firstborn, Esau. ”
(AU)
33 Isaac trembled violently and said, “Who was it, then, that hunted game and brought it
to me? I ate it just before you came and I blessed him—and indeed he will be
(AV)
blessed! ”
(AW)
34 When Esau heard his father’s words, he burst out with a loud and bitter cry and
(AX)
said to his father, “Bless me—me too, my father!”
(AY)
35 But he said, “Your brother came deceitfully and took your blessing.”
(AZ) (BA)
36 Esau said, “Isn’t he rightly named Jacob ? This is the second time he has taken
[a] (BB)
advantage of me: He took my birthright, and now he’s taken my blessing!” Then
(BC) (BD) (BE)
he asked, “Haven’t you reserved any blessing for me?”
37 Isaac answered Esau, “I have made him lord over you and have made all his relatives
his servants, and I have sustained him with grain and new wine. So what can I
(BF)
possibly do for you, my son?”
38 Esau said to his father, “Do you have only one blessing, my father? Bless me too, my
father!” Then Esau wept aloud.
(BG)
39 His father Isaac answered him,
(BH)
“Your dwelling will be
away from the earth’s richness,
away from the dew of heaven above.
(BI) (BJ)
40

You will live by the sword
and you will serve your brother.
(BK) (BL)
But when you grow restless,
you will throw his yoke
from off your neck. ”
(BM)
41 Esau held a grudge against Jacob because of the blessing his father had given
(BN) (BO)
him. He said to himself, “The days of mourning for my father are near; then I will
(BP)
kill my brother Jacob.”
(BQ) (BR)
42 When Rebekah was told what her older son Esau had said, she sent for her
(BS)
younger son Jacob and said to him, “Your brother Esau is planning to avenge himself by
killing you. 43 Now then, my son, do what I say: Flee at once to my brother
(BT) (BU)
Laban in Harran. 44 Stay with him for a while until your brother’s fury subsides.
(BV) (BW) (BX)
45 When your brother is no longer angry with you and forgets what you did to him, I’ll
(BY)
send word for you to come back from there. Why should I lose both of you in one
(BZ)
day?”
46 Then Rebekah said to Isaac, “I’m disgusted with living because of these Hittite
(CA)
women. If Jacob takes a wife from among the women of this land, from Hittite
(CB)
women like these, my life will not be worth living.”
(CC)

Genesis 29

Jacob Arrives in Paddan Aram
29 Then Jacob continued on his journey and came to the land of the eastern peoples.
(A)
2 There he saw a well in the open country, with three flocks of sheep lying near it
because the flocks were watered from that well. The stone over the mouth of the
(B) (C)
well was large. 3 When all the flocks were gathered there, the shepherds would roll the
stone away from the well’s mouth and water the sheep. Then they would return the
(D) (E)
stone to its place over the mouth of the well.
4 Jacob asked the shepherds, “My brothers, where are you from?”
(F)
“We’re from Harran, ” they replied.
(G)
5 He said to them, “Do you know Laban, Nahor’s grandson?”
(H)
“Yes, we know him,” they answered.
6 Then Jacob asked them, “Is he well?”
“Yes, he is,” they said, “and here comes his daughter Rachel with the sheep. ”
(I) (J)
7 “Look,” he said, “the sun is still high; it is not time for the flocks to be gathered. Water
the sheep and take them back to pasture.”
8 “We can’t,” they replied, “until all the flocks are gathered and the stone has been
(K)
rolled away from the mouth of the well. Then we will water the sheep.”
(L)
9 While he was still talking with them, Rachel came with her father’s sheep, for she
(M)
was a shepherd. 10 When Jacob saw Rachel daughter of his uncle Laban, and Laban’s
(N)
sheep, he went over and rolled the stone away from the mouth of the well and
(O)
watered his uncle’s sheep. 11 Then Jacob kissed Rachel and began to weep
(P) (Q) (R)

aloud. 12 He had told Rachel that he was a relative of her father and a son of
(S) (T)
Rebekah. So she ran and told her father.
(U) (V)
13 As soon as Laban heard the news about Jacob, his sister’s son, he hurried to meet
(W)
him. He embraced him and kissed him and brought him to his home, and there Jacob
(X)
told him all these things. 14 Then Laban said to him, “You are my own flesh and
blood.”
(Y)
Jacob Marries Leah and Rachel
After Jacob had stayed with him for a whole month, 15 Laban said to him, “Just
because you are a relative of mine, should you work for me for nothing? Tell me what
(Z)
your wages should be.”
(AA)
16 Now Laban had two daughters; the name of the older was Leah, and the name of
(AB)
the younger was Rachel. 17 Leah had weak eyes, but Rachel had a lovely figure
(AC) [a] (AD)
and was beautiful. 18 Jacob was in love with Rachel and said, “I’ll work for you
(AE) (AF)
seven years in return for your younger daughter Rachel.”
(AG)
19 Laban said, “It’s better that I give her to you than to some other man. Stay here with
me.” 20 So Jacob served seven years to get Rachel, but they seemed like only a few
(AH)
days to him because of his love for her.
(AI)
21 Then Jacob said to Laban, “Give me my wife. My time is completed, and I want to
make love to her. ”
(AJ)
22 So Laban brought together all the people of the place and gave a feast. 23 But
(AK)
when evening came, he took his daughter Leah and brought her to Jacob, and Jacob
(AL)

(AY)
he enabled her to conceive,

but

Genesis 32
Jacob Prepares to Meet Esau
32 Jacob also went on his way, and the angels of God met him. 2 When Jacob saw
[a] (A)
them, he said, “This is the camp of God!” So he named that place Mahanaim.
(B) [b](C)
3 Jacob sent messengers ahead of him to his brother Esau in the land of Seir, the
(D) (E) (F)
country of Edom. 4 He instructed them: “This is what you are to say to my lord Esau:
(G) (H)
‘Your servant Jacob says, I have been staying with Laban and have remained there till
(I) (J)
now. 5 I have cattle and donkeys, sheep and goats, male and female servants. Now I
(K)
am sending this message to my lord, that I may find favor in your eyes. ’”
(L) (M)
6 When the messengers returned to Jacob, they said, “We went to your brother Esau,
and now he is coming to meet you, and four hundred men are with him.”
(N)

7 In great fear and distress Jacob divided the people who were with him into two
(O) (P)
groups, and the flocks and herds and camels as well. 8 He thought, “If Esau comes
[c](Q)
and attacks one group, the group that is left may escape.”
[d] [e]
9 Then Jacob prayed, “O God of my father Abraham,
(R)
God of my father Isaac,
(S)
LORD,
you who said to me, ‘Go back to your country and your relatives, and I will make you
prosper,’ 10 I am unworthy of all the kindness and faithfulness you have shown your
(T) (U)
servant. I had only my staff when I crossed this Jordan, but now I have become two
(V)
camps. 11 Save me, I pray, from the hand of my brother Esau, for I am afraid he will
(W) (X)
come and attack me, and also the mothers with their children. 12 But you have said,
(Y) (Z)
‘I will surely make you prosper and will make your descendants like the sand of the
(AA)
sea, which cannot be counted. ’”
(AB)
13 He spent the night there, and from what he had with him he selected a gift for his
(AC)
brother Esau: 14 two hundred female goats and twenty male goats, two hundred ewes
and twenty rams, 15 thirty female camels with their young, forty cows and ten bulls,
(AD)
and twenty female donkeys and ten male donkeys. 16 He put them in the care of his
(AE)
servants, each herd by itself, and said to his servants, “Go ahead of me, and keep some
space between the herds.”
(AF)
17 He instructed the one in the lead: “When my brother Esau meets you and asks, ‘Who
do you belong to, and where are you going, and who owns all these animals in front of
you?’ 18 then you are to say, ‘They belong to your servant Jacob. They are a gift
(AG) (AH)
sent to my lord Esau, and he is coming behind us.’”
19 He also instructed the second, the third and all the others who followed the herds:
“You are to say the same thing to Esau when you meet him. 20 And be sure to say, ‘Your
servant Jacob is coming behind us.’” For he thought, “I will pacify him with these
(AI)
gifts I am sending on ahead; later, when I see him, perhaps he will receive me.”
(AJ) (AK) (AL)

21 So Jacob’s gifts went on ahead of him, but he himself spent the night in the
(AM)
camp.
Jacob Wrestles With God
22 That night Jacob got up and took his two wives, his two female servants and his
eleven sons and crossed the ford of the Jabbok. 23 After he had sent them across
(AN) (AO)
the stream, he sent over all his possessions. 24 So Jacob was left alone, and a
(AP) (AQ)
man wrestled with him till daybreak. 25 When the man saw that he could not
(AR)
overpower him, he touched the socket of Jacob’s hip so that his hip was wrenched as
(AS)
he wrestled with the man. 26 Then the man said, “Let me go, for it is daybreak.”
But Jacob replied, “I will not let you go unless you bless me.”
(AT)
27 The man asked him, “What is your name?”
“Jacob,” he answered.
(AU)
28 Then the man said, “Your name will no longer be Jacob, but Israel, because
(AV) [f](AW)
you have struggled with God and with humans and have overcome.”
(AX)
29 Jacob said, “Please tell me your name.”
(AY)
But he replied, “Why do you ask my name?” Then he blessed him there.
(AZ) (BA)
30 So Jacob called the place Peniel, saying, “It is because I saw God face to face,
[g] (BB)
and yet my life was spared.”
31 The sun rose above him as he passed Peniel, and he was limping because of his
[h](BC)
hip. 32 Therefore to this day the Israelites do not eat the tendon attached to the socket
of the hip, because the socket of Jacob’s hip was touched near the tendon.
(BD)

Genesis 33
Jacob Meets Esau
33 Jacob looked up and there was Esau, coming with his four hundred men; so he
(A)
divided the children among Leah, Rachel and the two female servants. 2 He put the
(B)
female servants and their children in front, Leah and her children next, and Rachel and
(C)
Joseph in the rear. 3 He himself went on ahead and bowed down to the ground
(D) (E)
seven times as he approached his brother.
(F)
4 But Esau ran to meet Jacob and embraced him; he threw his arms around his neck
(G)
and kissed him. And they wept. 5 Then Esau looked up and saw the women and
(H) (I)
children. “Who are these with you?” he asked.
Jacob answered, “They are the children God has graciously given your servant. ”
(J)
6 Then the female servants and their children approached and bowed down. 7 Next,
(K) (L)
Leah and her children came and bowed down. Last of all came Joseph and
(M) (N)

(O)

8 Esau asked, “What’s the meaning of all these flocks and herds I met?”
(P)
“To find favor in your eyes, my lord,” he said.
(Q)
9 But Esau said, “I already have plenty, my brother. Keep what you have for yourself.”
(R)

(S) (T)

(U)

(V) (W)

(X) (Y) (Z)

12 Then Esau said, “Let us be on our way; I’ll accompany you.”
13 But Jacob said to him, “My lord knows that the children are tender and that I must
(AA)

(AB)

(AC)
children, until I come to my lord in Seir. ”
(AD)
15 Esau said, “Then let me leave some of my men with you.”
“But why do that?” Jacob asked. “Just let me find favor in the eyes of my lord.”
(AE)

Genesis 34
Dinah and the Shechemites

34 Now Dinah, the daughter Leah had borne to Jacob, went out to visit the women of
(A)
the land. 2 When Shechem son of Hamor the Hivite, the ruler of that area, saw her,
(B) (C) (D)
he took her and raped her. 3 His heart was drawn to Dinah daughter of Jacob; he
(E) (F) (G)
loved the young woman and spoke tenderly to her. 4 And Shechem said to his father
(H) (I)
Hamor, “Get me this girl as my wife.”
(J)
5 When Jacob heard that his daughter Dinah had been defiled, his sons were in the
(K)
fields with his livestock; so he did nothing about it until they came home.
6 Then Shechem’s father Hamor went out to talk with Jacob. 7 Meanwhile, Jacob’s
(L)
sons had come in from the fields as soon as they heard what had happened. They were
shocked and furious, because Shechem had done an outrageous thing in Israel
(M) (N) [a] (O)
by sleeping with Jacob’s daughter—a thing that should not be done.
(P)
8 But Hamor said to them, “My son Shechem has his heart set on your daughter. Please
give her to him as his wife. 9 Intermarry with us; give us your daughters and take our
(Q)
daughters for yourselves. 10 You can settle among us; the land is open to you.
(R) (S) (T)
Live in it, trade in it, and acquire property in it. ”
[b] (U) (V)

(W)

(X)

(Y) (Z)
they spoke to Shechem and his father Hamor. 14 They said to them, “We can’t do such a

(AA)

(AB)

(AC)

(AD)

one people with you. 17 But if you will not agree to be circumcised, we’ll take our
(AE)
sister and go.”
18 Their proposal seemed good to Hamor and his son Shechem. 19 The young man,
who was the most honored of all his father’s family, lost no time in doing what they
(AF)
said, because he was delighted with Jacob’s daughter. 20 So Hamor and his son
(AG)

(AH)
men are friendly toward us,” they said. “Let them live in our land and trade in it; the
(AI)

ours. 22 But the men will agree to live with us as one people only on the condition
(AJ)

(AL)

Genesis 35
Jacob Returns to Bethel
35 Then God said to Jacob, “Go up to Bethel and settle there, and build an altar there
(A) (B)
to God, who appeared to you when you were fleeing from your brother Esau.”
(C) (D) (E)
2 So Jacob said to his household and to all who were with him, “Get rid of the foreign
(F)
gods you have with you, and purify yourselves and change your clothes. 3 Then
(G) (H)
come, let us go up to Bethel, where I will build an altar to God, who answered me in the
(I)
day of my distress and who has been with me wherever I have gone. ” 4 So they gave
(J) (K)
Jacob all the foreign gods they had and the rings in their ears, and Jacob buried them
(L)
under the oak at Shechem. 5 Then they set out, and the terror of God fell on the
(M) (N) (O)
towns all around them so that no one pursued them.
(P)
6 Jacob and all the people with him came to Luz (that is, Bethel) in the land of
(Q)
Canaan. 7 There he built an altar, and he called the place El Bethel, because it
(R) (S) [a](T)
was there that God revealed himself to him when he was fleeing from his brother.
(U) (V)
8 Now Deborah, Rebekah’s nurse, died and was buried under the oak outside
(W) (X)
Bethel. So it was named Allon Bakuth.
(Y) [b]

9 After Jacob returned from Paddan Aram, God appeared to him again and blessed
[c](Z)
him. 10 God said to him, “Your name is Jacob, but you will no longer be called
(AA) [d]
Jacob; your name will be Israel. ” So he named him Israel.
[e] (AB)
11 And God said to him, “I am God Almighty ; be fruitful and increase in number. A
[f](AC) (AD)
nation and a community of nations will come from you, and kings will be among your
(AE)
descendants. 12 The land I gave to Abraham and Isaac I also give to you, and I will
(AF)
give this land to your descendants after you. ” 13 Then God went up from him at
(AG) (AH) (AI)
the place where he had talked with him.
14 Jacob set up a stone pillar at the place where God had talked with him, and he
(AJ)
poured out a drink offering on it; he also poured oil on it. 15 Jacob called the place
(AK) (AL)
where God had talked with him Bethel.
[g](AM)
The Deaths of Rachel and Isaac(AN)
16 Then they moved on from Bethel. While they were still some distance from
Ephrath, Rachel began to give birth and had great difficulty. 17 And as she was
(AO) (AP)
having great difficulty in childbirth, the midwife said to her, “Don’t despair, for you
(AQ)
have another son.” 18 As she breathed her last—for she was dying—she named her
(AR)
son Ben-Oni. But his father named him Benjamin.
[h](AS) [i](AT)
19 So Rachel died and was buried on the way to Ephrath (that is, Bethlehem ). 20
(AU) (AV)
Over her tomb Jacob set up a pillar, and to this day that pillar marks Rachel’s
(AW)
tomb.
(AX)
21 Israel moved on again and pitched his tent beyond Migdal Eder. 22 While Israel
(AY)
was living in that region, Reuben went in and slept with his father’s concubine
(AZ)
Bilhah, and Israel heard of it.
(BA)
Jacob had twelve sons:

23 The sons of Leah:

Footnotes
a.  Genesis 35:7 El Bethel means God of Bethel.
b.  Genesis 35:8 Allon Bakuth means oak of weeping.
c.  Genesis 35:9 That is, Northwest Mesopotamia; also in verse 26
d.  Genesis 35:10 Jacob means he grasps the heel, a Hebrew idiom for he deceives.
e.  Genesis 35:10 Israel probably means he struggles with God.

f. Genesis 35:11 Hebrew El-Shaddai
g. Genesis 35:15 Bethel means house of God.
h. Genesis 35:18 Ben-Oni means son of my trouble.
i. Genesis 35:18 Benjamin means son of my right hand.

31 These were the kings who reigned in Edom before any Israelite king reigned:
(BD)

(BE)

(BF)

(BG)

(BH)
36 When Hadad died, Samlah from Masrekah succeeded him as king.
37 When Samlah died, Shaul from Rehoboth on the river succeeded him as king.
(BI)
38 When Shaul died, Baal-Hanan son of Akbor succeeded him as king.
39 When Baal-Hanan son of Akbor died, Hadad succeeded him as king. His city
[e]
was named Pau, and his wife’s name was Mehetabel daughter of Matred, the
daughter of Me-Zahab.
40 These were the chiefs descended from Esau, by name, according to their clans
(BJ)
and regions:

Timna, Alvah, Jetheth, 41 Oholibamah, Elah, Pinon, 42 Kenaz, Teman, Mibzar, 43
Magdiel and Iram. These were the chiefs of Edom, according to their settlements in
the land they occupied.
This is the family line of Esau, the father of the Edomites.
(BK)

Genesis 37
Joseph’s Dreams
37 Jacob lived in the land where his father had stayed, the land of Canaan.
(A) (B)
2 This is the account of Jacob’s family line.
(C)

Joseph, a young man of seventeen, was tending the flocks with his brothers, the
(D) (E) (F)
sons of Bilhah and the sons of Zilpah, his father’s wives, and he brought their father
(G) (H)
a bad report about them.
(I)
3 Now Israel loved Joseph more than any of his other sons, because he had been
(J) (K)
born to him in his old age; and he made an ornate robe for him. 4 When his
(L) [a] (M) (N)
brothers saw that their father loved him more than any of them, they hated him and
(O)
could not speak a kind word to him.
5 Joseph had a dream, and when he told it to his brothers, they hated him all the
(P) (Q)
more. 6 He said to them, “Listen to this dream I had: 7 We were binding sheaves of
(R) (S)
grain out in the field when suddenly my sheaf rose and stood upright, while your
sheaves gathered around mine and bowed down to it.”
(T)
8 His brothers said to him, “Do you intend to reign over us? Will you actually rule us?”
(U)
And they hated him all the more because of his dream and what he had said.
(V)
9 Then he had another dream, and he told it to his brothers. “Listen,” he said, “I had
(W)
another dream, and this time the sun and moon and eleven stars were bowing down to
(X)
me.”
(Y)
10 When he told his father as well as his brothers, his father rebuked him and said,
(Z) (AA)
“What is this dream you had? Will your mother and I and your brothers actually come
and bow down to the ground before you?” 11 His brothers were jealous of him, but
(AB) (AC)
his father kept the matter in mind.
(AD)
Joseph Sold by His Brothers
12 Now his brothers had gone to graze their father’s flocks near Shechem, 13 and
(AE)
Israel said to Joseph, “As you know, your brothers are grazing the flocks near
(AF)
Shechem. Come, I am going to send you to them.”
(AG)

“Very well,” he replied.
14 So he said to him, “Go and see if all is well with your brothers and with the flocks,
(AH)
and bring word back to me.” Then he sent him off from the Valley of Hebron.
(AI)
When Joseph arrived at Shechem, 15 a man found him wandering around in the fields
and asked him, “What are you looking for?”
16 He replied, “I’m looking for my brothers. Can you tell me where they are grazing their
flocks?”
17 “They have moved on from here,” the man answered. “I heard them say, ‘Let’s go to
Dothan. ’”
(AJ)
So Joseph went after his brothers and found them near Dothan. 18 But they saw him in
the distance, and before he reached them, they plotted to kill him.
(AK)
19 “Here comes that dreamer! ” they said to each other. 20 “Come now, let’s kill him
(AL)
and throw him into one of these cisterns and say that a ferocious animal
(AM) (AN)
devoured him. Then we’ll see what comes of his dreams.”
(AO) (AP)
21 When Reuben heard this, he tried to rescue him from their hands. “Let’s not take
(AQ)
his life,” he said. 22 “Don’t shed any blood. Throw him into this cistern here in the
(AR) (AS)
wilderness, but don’t lay a hand on him.” Reuben said this to rescue him from them and
take him back to his father.
(AT)
23 So when Joseph came to his brothers, they stripped him of his robe—the ornate
robe he was wearing— 24 and they took him and threw him into the cistern. The
(AU) (AV)
cistern was empty; there was no water in it.

25 As they sat down to eat their meal, they looked up and saw a caravan of
Ishmaelites coming from Gilead. Their camels were loaded with spices, balm
(AW) (AX) (AY)
and myrrh, and they were on their way to take them down to Egypt.

(BB)
his blood? 27 Come, let’s sell him to the Ishmaelites and not lay our hands on him;

after all, he is our brother, our own flesh and blood. ” His brothers agreed.
(BD) (BE)

(BF)
the cistern and sold him for twenty shekels of silver to the Ishmaelites, who
(BG) (BH) [b] (BI) (BJ)
took him to Egypt.

(BM)
31 Then they got Joseph’s robe, slaughtered a goat and dipped the robe in the

blood. 32 They took the ornate robe back to their father and said, “We found this.

(BQ)

Genesis 38
Judah and Tamar
38 At that time, Judah left his brothers and went down to stay with a man of
(A)
Adullam named Hirah. 2 There Judah met the daughter of a Canaanite man named
(B) (C)
Shua. He married her and made love to her; 3 she became pregnant and gave birth to
(D)
a son, who was named Er. 4 She conceived again and gave birth to a son and named
(E)
him Onan. 5 She gave birth to still another son and named him Shelah. It was at
(F) (G)
Kezib that she gave birth to him.
6 Judah got a wife for Er, his firstborn, and her name was Tamar. 7 But Er, Judah’s
(H)
firstborn, was wicked in the LORD’s sight;
(I)
so the LORD put him to death.
(J)
8 Then Judah said to Onan, “Sleep with your brother’s wife and fulfill your duty to her as
a brother-in-law to raise up offspring for your brother.” 9 But Onan knew that the child
(K)
would not be his; so whenever he slept with his brother’s wife, he spilled his semen on
the ground to keep from providing offspring for his brother. 10 What he did was wicked
in the LORD’s sight; so the LORD put him to death also.
(L)
11 Judah then said to his daughter-in-law Tamar, “Live as a widow in your father’s
(M) (N)
household until my son Shelah grows up.” For he thought, “He may die too, just
(O) (P) (Q)
like his brothers.” So Tamar went to live in her father’s household.
12 After a long time Judah’s wife, the daughter of Shua, died. When Judah had
(R)
recovered from his grief, he went up to Timnah, to the men who were shearing his
(S)
sheep, and his friend Hirah the Adullamite went with him.
(T) (U)

13 When Tamar was told, “Your father-in-law is on his way to Timnah to shear his
(V)
sheep,” 14 she took off her widow’s clothes, covered herself with a veil to disguise
(W) (X) (Y)
herself, and then sat down at the entrance to Enaim, which is on the road to
(Z)
Timnah. For she saw that, though Shelah had now grown up, she had not been
(AA) (AB)
given to him as his wife.
15 When Judah saw her, he thought she was a prostitute, for she had covered her
(AC)
face. 16 Not realizing that she was his daughter-in-law, he went over to her by the
(AD) (AE)
roadside and said, “Come now, let me sleep with you.”
(AF)
“And what will you give me to sleep with you?” she asked.
(AG)
17 “I’ll send you a young goat from my flock,” he said.
(AH)
“Will you give me something as a pledge until you send it?” she asked.
(AI)
18 He said, “What pledge should I give you?”
“Your seal and its cord, and the staff in your hand,” she answered. So he gave them
(AJ) (AK)
to her and slept with her, and she became pregnant by him. 19 After she left, she took
(AL)
off her veil and put on her widow’s clothes again.
(AM)
20 Meanwhile Judah sent the young goat by his friend the Adullamite in order to get
(AN)
his pledge back from the woman, but he did not find her. 21 He asked the men who
(AO)
lived there, “Where is the shrine prostitute who was beside the road at Enaim?”
(AP)
“There hasn’t been any shrine prostitute here,” they said.
22 So he went back to Judah and said, “I didn’t find her. Besides, the men who lived
there said, ‘There hasn’t been any shrine prostitute here.’”

23 Then Judah said, “Let her keep what she has, or we will become a
(AQ)
laughingstock. After all, I did send her this young goat, but you didn’t find her.”
(AR)
24 About three months later Judah was told, “Your daughter-in-law Tamar is guilty of
prostitution, and as a result she is now pregnant.”
Judah said, “Bring her out and have her burned to death!”
(AS)
25 As she was being brought out, she sent a message to her father-in-law. “I am
pregnant by the man who owns these,” she said. And she added, “See if you recognize
whose seal and cord and staff these are.”
(AT)
26 Judah recognized them and said, “She is more righteous than I, since I wouldn’t
(AU)
give her to my son Shelah. ” And he did not sleep with her again.
(AV)
27 When the time came for her to give birth, there were twin boys in her womb. 28 As
(AW)
she was giving birth, one of them put out his hand; so the midwife took a scarlet
(AX)
thread and tied it on his wrist and said, “This one came out first.” 29 But when he
(AY)
drew back his hand, his brother came out, and she said, “So this is how you have
(AZ)
broken out!” And he was named Perez. 30 Then his brother, who had the scarlet
[a](BA)
thread on his wrist, came out. And he was named Zerah.
(BB) [b](BC)

Genesis 39
Joseph and Potiphar’s Wife

39 Now Joseph had been taken down to Egypt. Potiphar, an Egyptian who was one of
(A)
Pharaoh’s officials, the captain of the guard, bought him from the Ishmaelites who had
(B)
taken him there.
(C)
2 The LORD was with Joseph
(D)
so that he prospered, and he lived in the house of his
Egyptian master. 3 When his master saw that the LORD was with him
(E)
and that the LORD
gave him success in everything he did, 4 Joseph found favor in his eyes and became
(F) (G)
his attendant. Potiphar put him in charge of his household, and he entrusted to his
(H)
care everything he owned. 5 From the time he put him in charge of his household and
(I)
of all that he owned, the LORD blessed the household
(J)
of the Egyptian because of
Joseph.
(K)
The blessing of the LORD was on everything Potiphar had, both in the house
and in the field. 6 So Potiphar left everything he had in Joseph’s care; with Joseph in
(L) (M)
charge, he did not concern himself with anything except the food he ate.
Now Joseph was well-built and handsome, 7 and after a while his master’s wife took
(N)
notice of Joseph and said, “Come to bed with me!”
(O)
8 But he refused. “With me in charge,” he told her, “my master does not concern
(P)
himself with anything in the house; everything he owns he has entrusted to my care. 9
(Q)
No one is greater in this house than I am. My master has withheld nothing from me
(R)
except you, because you are his wife. How then could I do such a wicked thing and sin
against God?” 10 And though she spoke to Joseph day after day, he refused to go to
(S) (T)
bed with her or even be with her.
11 One day he went into the house to attend to his duties, and none of the household
(U)
servants was inside. 12 She caught him by his cloak and said, “Come to bed with
(V) (W)
me!” But he left his cloak in her hand and ran out of the house.
(X) (Y)
13 When she saw that he had left his cloak in her hand and had run out of the house, 14
she called her household servants. “Look,” she said to them, “this Hebrew has been
(Z) (AA)

brought to us to make sport of us! He came in here to sleep with me, but I
(AB)
screamed. 15 When he heard me scream for help, he left his cloak beside me and ran
(AC)
out of the house.”
(AD)
16 She kept his cloak beside her until his master came home. 17 Then she told him this
story: “That Hebrew slave you brought us came to me to make sport of me. 18
(AE) (AF) (AG)
But as soon as I screamed for help, he left his cloak beside me and ran out of the
house.”
19 When his master heard the story his wife told him, saying, “This is how your slave
treated me,” he burned with anger. 20 Joseph’s master took him and put him in
(AH)
prison, the place where the king’s prisoners were confined.
(AI)
But while Joseph was there in the prison, 21 the LORD was with him;
(AJ)
he showed him
kindness and granted him favor in the eyes of the prison warden. 22 So the warden
(AK) (AL)
put Joseph in charge of all those held in the prison, and he was made responsible for all
that was done there. 23 The warden paid no attention to anything under Joseph’s
(AM) (AN)
care, because the LORD was with Joseph and gave him success in whatever he did.
(AO)

Genesis 40
The Cupbearer and the Baker
40 Some time later, the cupbearer and the baker of the king of Egypt offended their
(A) (B)
master, the king of Egypt. 2 Pharaoh was angry with his two officials, the chief
(C) (D)
cupbearer and the chief baker, 3 and put them in custody in the house of the captain of
the guard, in the same prison where Joseph was confined. 4 The captain of the
(E)
guard assigned them to Joseph, and he attended them.
(F) (G)
After they had been in custody for some time, 5 each of the two men—the cupbearer
(H)
and the baker of the king of Egypt, who were being held in prison—had a dream the
(I)
same night, and each dream had a meaning of its own.
(J)
6 When Joseph came to them the next morning, he saw that they were dejected. 7 So he
asked Pharaoh’s officials who were in custody with him in his master’s house, “Why do
(K)
you look so sad today?”
(L)
8 “We both had dreams,” they answered, “but there is no one to interpret them.”
(M)
Then Joseph said to them, “Do not interpretations belong to God? Tell me your
(N)
dreams.”
9 So the chief cupbearer told Joseph his dream. He said to him, “In my dream I saw a
(O)
vine in front of me, 10 and on the vine were three branches. As soon as it budded, it
blossomed, and its clusters ripened into grapes. 11 Pharaoh’s cup was in my hand,
(P)
and I took the grapes, squeezed them into Pharaoh’s cup and put the cup in his hand.”

12 “This is what it means, ” Joseph said to him. “The three branches are three days.
(Q) (R)
13 Within three days Pharaoh will lift up your head and restore you to your position,
(S) (T)
and you will put Pharaoh’s cup in his hand, just as you used to do when you were his
cupbearer. 14 But when all goes well with you, remember me and show me
(U) (V)
kindness; mention me to Pharaoh and get me out of this prison. 15 I was forcibly
(W) (X)
carried off from the land of the Hebrews, and even here I have done nothing to
(Y)
deserve being put in a dungeon.”
(Z)
16 When the chief baker saw that Joseph had given a favorable interpretation, he
(AA) (AB)
said to Joseph, “I too had a dream: On my head were three baskets of bread. 17 In
(AC) [a]
the top basket were all kinds of baked goods for Pharaoh, but the birds were eating
them out of the basket on my head.”
18 “This is what it means,” Joseph said. “The three baskets are three days. 19 Within
(AD)
three days Pharaoh will lift off your head and impale your body on a pole. And
(AE) (AF) (AG)
the birds will eat away your flesh.”
(AH)
20 Now the third day was Pharaoh’s birthday, and he gave a feast for all his
(AI) (AJ)
officials. He lifted up the heads of the chief cupbearer and the chief baker in the
(AK) (AL)
presence of his officials: 21 He restored the chief cupbearer to his position, so
(AM) (AN)
that he once again put the cup into Pharaoh’s hand — 22 but he impaled the chief
(AO)
baker, just as Joseph had said to them in his interpretation.
(AP) (AQ)
23 The chief cupbearer, however, did not remember Joseph; he forgot him.
(AR)

Genesis 41
Pharaoh’s Dreams
41 When two full years had passed, Pharaoh had a dream: He was standing by the
(A)
Nile, 2 when out of the river there came up seven cows, sleek and fat, and they
(B) (C)
grazed among the reeds. 3 After them, seven other cows, ugly and gaunt, came up out
(D)
of the Nile and stood beside those on the riverbank. 4 And the cows that were ugly and
gaunt ate up the seven sleek, fat cows. Then Pharaoh woke up.
(E)
5 He fell asleep again and had a second dream: Seven heads of grain, healthy and
(F)
good, were growing on a single stalk. 6 After them, seven other heads of grain
sprouted—thin and scorched by the east wind. 7 The thin heads of grain swallowed up
(G)
the seven healthy, full heads. Then Pharaoh woke up; it had been a dream.
(H)

8 In the morning his mind was troubled, so he sent for all the magicians and wise
(I) (J)
men of Egypt. Pharaoh told them his dreams, but no one could interpret them for him.
(K)
9 Then the chief cupbearer said to Pharaoh, “Today I am reminded of my
shortcomings. 10 Pharaoh was once angry with his servants, and he imprisoned me
(L) (M)
and the chief baker in the house of the captain of the guard. 11 Each of us had a
(N)
dream the same night, and each dream had a meaning of its own. 12 Now a young
(O)
Hebrew was there with us, a servant of the captain of the guard. We told him our
(P) (Q)
dreams, and he interpreted them for us, giving each man the interpretation of his
dream. 13 And things turned out exactly as he interpreted them to us: I was restored
(R)
to my position, and the other man was impaled. ”
(S)
14 So Pharaoh sent for Joseph, and he was quickly brought from the dungeon. When
(T)
he had shaved and changed his clothes, he came before Pharaoh.
(U) (V)
15 Pharaoh said to Joseph, “I had a dream, and no one can interpret it. But I have
(W)
heard it said of you that when you hear a dream you can interpret it.”
(X)
16 “I cannot do it,” Joseph replied to Pharaoh, “but God will give Pharaoh the answer he
desires.”
(Y)
17 Then Pharaoh said to Joseph, “In my dream I was standing on the bank of the Nile,
(Z)
18 when out of the river there came up seven cows, fat and sleek, and they grazed
among the reeds. 19 After them, seven other cows came up—scrawny and very ugly
(AA)
and lean. I had never seen such ugly cows in all the land of Egypt. 20 The lean, ugly
cows ate up the seven fat cows that came up first. 21 But even after they ate them, no
one could tell that they had done so; they looked just as ugly as before. Then I woke up.
22 “In my dream I saw seven heads of grain, full and good, growing on a single stalk. 23
After them, seven other heads sprouted—withered and thin and scorched by the east

wind. 24 The thin heads of grain swallowed up the seven good heads. I told this to the
magicians, but none of them could explain it to me. ”
(AB)
25 Then Joseph said to Pharaoh, “The dreams of Pharaoh are one and the same. God
(AC)
has revealed to Pharaoh what he is about to do. 26 The seven good cows are
(AD) (AE)
seven years, and the seven good heads of grain are seven years; it is one and the same
dream. 27 The seven lean, ugly cows that came up afterward are seven years, and so
are the seven worthless heads of grain scorched by the east wind: They are seven years
of famine.
(AF)
28 “It is just as I said to Pharaoh: God has shown Pharaoh what he is about to do. 29
(AG)
Seven years of great abundance are coming throughout the land of Egypt, 30 but
(AH)
seven years of famine will follow them. Then all the abundance in Egypt will be
(AI)
forgotten, and the famine will ravage the land. 31 The abundance in the land will not
(AJ)
be remembered, because the famine that follows it will be so severe. 32 The reason the
dream was given to Pharaoh in two forms is that the matter has been firmly decided
(AK)
by God, and God will do it soon.
(AL)
33 “And now let Pharaoh look for a discerning and wise man and put him in charge
(AM)
of the land of Egypt. 34 Let Pharaoh appoint commissioners over the land to take
(AN) (AO)
a fifth of the harvest of Egypt during the seven years of abundance. 35 They
(AP) (AQ)
should collect all the food of these good years that are coming and store up the grain
under the authority of Pharaoh, to be kept in the cities for food. 36 This food should
(AR)
be held in reserve for the country, to be used during the seven years of famine that will
come upon Egypt, so that the country may not be ruined by the famine.”
(AS)
37 The plan seemed good to Pharaoh and to all his officials. 38 So Pharaoh asked
(AT)
them, “Can we find anyone like this man, one in whom is the spirit of God ?”
[a] (AU)

39 Then Pharaoh said to Joseph, “Since God has made all this known to you, there is
(AV)
no one so discerning and wise as you. 40 You shall be in charge of my palace, and
(AW) (AX)
all my people are to submit to your orders. Only with respect to the throne will I be
(AY)
greater than you. ”
(AZ)
Joseph in Charge of Egypt
41 So Pharaoh said to Joseph, “I hereby put you in charge of the whole land of Egypt.”
(BA)
42 Then Pharaoh took his signet ring from his finger and put it on Joseph’s finger. He
(BB)
dressed him in robes of fine linen and put a gold chain around his neck. 43 He
(BC) (BD) (BE)
had him ride in a chariot as his second-in-command, and people shouted before
(BF) [b](BG)
him, “Make way !” Thus he put him in charge of the whole land of Egypt.
[c] (BH) (BI)
44 Then Pharaoh said to Joseph, “I am Pharaoh, but without your word no one will lift
hand or foot in all Egypt.” 45 Pharaoh gave Joseph the name Zaphenath-Paneah
(BJ) (BK)
and gave him Asenath daughter of Potiphera, priest of On, to be his wife. And
(BL) [d](BM) (BN)
Joseph went throughout the land of Egypt.
46 Joseph was thirty years old when he entered the service of Pharaoh king of
(BO) (BP)
Egypt. And Joseph went out from Pharaoh’s presence and traveled throughout Egypt. 47
During the seven years of abundance the land produced plentifully. 48 Joseph
(BQ)
collected all the food produced in those seven years of abundance in Egypt and stored it
in the cities. In each city he put the food grown in the fields surrounding it. 49 Joseph
(BR)
stored up huge quantities of grain, like the sand of the sea; it was so much that he
(BS)
stopped keeping records because it was beyond measure.
50 Before the years of famine came, two sons were born to Joseph by Asenath
daughter of Potiphera, priest of On. 51 Joseph named his firstborn Manasseh
(BT) (BU) [e](BV)
and said, “It is because God has made me forget all my trouble and all my father’s
household.” 52 The second son he named Ephraim and said, “It is because God has
[f](BW)
made me fruitful in the land of my suffering.”
(BX)

53 The seven years of abundance in Egypt came to an end, 54 and the seven years of
famine began, just as Joseph had said. There was famine in all the other lands, but
(BY) (BZ)
in the whole land of Egypt there was food. 55 When all Egypt began to feel the
famine, the people cried to Pharaoh for food. Then Pharaoh told all the Egyptians, “Go
(CA)
to Joseph and do what he tells you.”
(CB)
56 When the famine had spread over the whole country, Joseph opened all the
storehouses and sold grain to the Egyptians, for the famine was severe throughout
(CC) (CD)
Egypt. 57 And all the world came to Egypt to buy grain from Joseph, because the
(CE) (CF)
famine was severe everywhere.
(CG)

Genesis 42
Joseph’s Brothers Go to Egypt
42 When Jacob learned that there was grain in Egypt, he said to his sons, “Why do you
(A)
just keep looking at each other?” 2 He continued, “I have heard that there is grain in
Egypt. Go down there and buy some for us, so that we may live and not die.”
(B) (C)

3 Then ten of Joseph’s brothers went down to buy grain from Egypt. 4 But Jacob did
(D)
not send Benjamin, Joseph’s brother, with the others, because he was afraid that harm
(E)
might come to him. 5 So Israel’s sons were among those who went to buy grain, for
(F) (G)
there was famine in the land of Canaan also.
(H) (I)
6 Now Joseph was the governor of the land, the person who sold grain to all its
(J)
people. So when Joseph’s brothers arrived, they bowed down to him with their faces
(K)
to the ground. 7 As soon as Joseph saw his brothers, he recognized them, but he
(L)
pretended to be a stranger and spoke harshly to them. “Where do you come from?”
(M) (N)
he asked.
“From the land of Canaan,” they replied, “to buy food.”
8 Although Joseph recognized his brothers, they did not recognize him. 9 Then he
(O)
remembered his dreams about them and said to them, “You are spies! You have
(P) (Q)
come to see where our land is unprotected.”
(R)
10 “No, my lord, ” they answered. “Your servants have come to buy food. 11 We are
(S) (T)
all the sons of one man. Your servants are honest men, not spies. ”
(U) (V) (W)
12 “No!” he said to them. “You have come to see where our land is unprotected.”
(X)
13 But they replied, “Your servants were twelve brothers, the sons of one man, who
(Y)
lives in the land of Canaan. The youngest is now with our father, and one is no
(Z)
more.”
(AA)
14 Joseph said to them, “It is just as I told you: You are spies! 15 And this is how you
(AB)
will be tested: As surely as Pharaoh lives, you will not leave this place unless your
(AC)
youngest brother comes here. 16 Send one of your number to get your brother; the

(AR) (AS) (AT)

(AW)

(AX)
sack, and to give them provisions for their journey. After this was done for them,
(AY) (AZ) (BA)
26 they loaded their grain on their donkeys and left.
(BB)
27 At the place where they stopped for the night one of them opened his sack to get

(BC) (BD)
has been returned,” he said to his brothers. “Here it is in my sack.”
Their hearts sank and they turned to each other trembling and said, “What is this
(BE) (BF)
that God has done to us?”
(BG)

29 When they came to their father Jacob in the land of Canaan, they told him all that
(BH)
had happened to them. They said, 30 “The man who is lord over the land spoke
(BI)
harshly to us and treated us as though we were spying on the land. 31 But we said
(BJ) (BK)
to him, ‘We are honest men; we are not spies. 32 We were twelve brothers, sons of
(BL)
one father. One is no more, and the youngest is now with our father in Canaan.’
(BM)
33 “Then the man who is lord over the land said to us, ‘This is how I will know whether

(BN)
that you are not spies but honest men. Then I will give your brother back to you,

Genesis 43
The Second Journey to Egypt
43 Now the famine was still severe in the land. 2 So when they had eaten all the grain
(A)
they had brought from Egypt, their father said to them, “Go back and buy us a little
(B)
more food.”
(C)
3 But Judah said to him, “The man warned us solemnly, ‘You will not see my face
(D)
again unless your brother is with you.’ 4 If you will send our brother along with us, we
(E)

will go down and buy food for you. 5 But if you will not send him, we will not go down,
(F)
because the man said to us, ‘You will not see my face again unless your brother is with
you. ’”
(G)
6 Israel asked, “Why did you bring this trouble on me by telling the man you had
(H) (I)
another brother?”
7 They replied, “The man questioned us closely about ourselves and our family. ‘Is your
father still living?’ he asked us. ‘Do you have another brother?’ We simply answered
(J) (K)
his questions. How were we to know he would say, ‘Bring your brother down here’?”
(L)
8 Then Judah said to Israel his father, “Send the boy along with me and we will go at
(M) (N)
once, so that we and you and our children may live and not die. 9 I myself will
(O)
guarantee his safety; you can hold me personally responsible for him. If I do not bring
(P)
him back to you and set him here before you, I will bear the blame before you all my
(Q)
life. 10 As it is, if we had not delayed, we could have gone and returned twice.”
(R) (S)
11 Then their father Israel said to them, “If it must be, then do this: Put some of the
(T)
best products of the land in your bags and take them down to the man as a gift —a
(U) (V)
little balm and a little honey, some spices and myrrh, some pistachio nuts and
(W) (X) (Y)
almonds. 12 Take double the amount of silver with you, for you must return the silver
(Z)
that was put back into the mouths of your sacks. Perhaps it was a mistake. 13 Take
(AA)
your brother also and go back to the man at once. 14 And may God Almighty
(AB) [a](AC)
grant you mercy before the man so that he will let your other brother and Benjamin
(AD)
come back with you. As for me, if I am bereaved, I am bereaved.”
(AE) (AF)
15 So the men took the gifts and double the amount of silver, and Benjamin also.
(AG)
They hurried down to Egypt and presented themselves to Joseph. 16 When Joseph
(AH) (AI)
saw Benjamin with them, he said to the steward of his house, “Take these men to
(AJ) (AK)
my house, slaughter an animal and prepare a meal; they are to eat with me at noon.”
(AL)

17 The man did as Joseph told him and took the men to Joseph’s house. 18 Now the
(AM)
men were frightened when they were taken to his house. They thought, “We were
(AN) (AO)
brought here because of the silver that was put back into our sacks the first time. He
(AP)
wants to attack us and overpower us and seize us as slaves and take our
(AQ) (AR)
donkeys. ”

19 So they went up to Joseph’s steward and spoke to him at the entrance to the
(AT)
house. 20 “We beg your pardon, our lord,” they said, “we came down here the first time

and each of us found his silver—the exact weight—in the mouth of his sack. So we have

(AV)

(AW)
you treasure in your sacks; I received your silver.” Then he brought Simeon out to
(AX)
them.
(AY)
24 The steward took the men into Joseph’s house, gave them water to wash their
(AZ)
feet and provided fodder for their donkeys. 25 They prepared their gifts for

(BD) (BE)

(BI)
down, prostrating themselves before him.

gracious to you, my son.” 30 Deeply moved at the sight of his brother, Joseph
(BN) (BO)
