(BP)
there.
31 After he had washed his face, he came out and, controlling himself, said, “Serve
(BQ)
the food.”

with him.

Genesis 44
A Silver Cup in a Sack
44 Now Joseph gave these instructions to the steward of his house: “Fill the men’s
(A)
sacks with as much food as they can carry, and put each man’s silver in the mouth of his
sack. 2 Then put my cup, the silver one, in the mouth of the youngest one’s sack,
(B) (C) (D)
along with the silver for his grain.” And he did as Joseph said.
3 As morning dawned, the men were sent on their way with their donkeys. 4 They had
(E)
not gone far from the city when Joseph said to his steward, “Go after those men at
(F)
once, and when you catch up with them, say to them, ‘Why have you repaid good with

(G) (H) (I)
is a wicked thing you have done.’”
6 When he caught up with them, he repeated these words to them. 7 But they said to

(J)
like that! 8 We even brought back to you from the land of Canaan the silver we
(K) (L) (M)

(N) (O)

(P) (Q)

(R)

(S)

(T) (U)

(AB) (AC)

(AI) (AJ)

(AM)

(AX)

Genesis 45
Joseph Makes Himself Known
45 Then Joseph could no longer control himself before all his attendants, and he cried
(A)
out, “Have everyone leave my presence!” So there was no one with Joseph when he
(B)
made himself known to his brothers. 2 And he wept so loudly that the Egyptians heard
(C)
him, and Pharaoh’s household heard about it.
(D)
3 Joseph said to his brothers, “I am Joseph! Is my father still living?” But his brothers
(E)
were not able to answer him, because they were terrified at his presence.
(F) (G)
4 Then Joseph said to his brothers, “Come close to me.” When they had done so, he
(H)
said, “I am your brother Joseph, the one you sold into Egypt! 5 And now, do not be
(I)
distressed and do not be angry with yourselves for selling me here, because it was
(J) (K)
to save lives that God sent me ahead of you. 6 For two years now there has been
(L)
famine in the land, and for the next five years there will be no plowing and reaping. 7
(M)
But God sent me ahead of you to preserve for you a remnant on earth and to save
(N)
your lives by a great deliverance.
[a](O)

(P) (Q)
Pharaoh, lord of his entire household and ruler of all Egypt. 9 Now hurry back to my
(R) (S)

(T) (U)

(V) (W) (X)

(Y)
12 “You can see for yourselves, and so can my brother Benjamin, that it is really I who
(Z)

(AA) (AB)

(AC)

(AD)
embraced him, weeping. 15 And he kissed all his brothers and wept over them.
(AE) (AF) (AG)

(AH)

(AI)
Pharaoh and all his officials were pleased. 17 Pharaoh said to Joseph, “Tell your

brothers, ‘Do this: Load your animals and return to the land of Canaan, 18 and

(AN) (AO)

(AQ) (AR)

(AS)

(AU) [b]

(AV) (AW)

(AX)
and other provisions for his journey. 24 Then he sent his brothers away, and as they
(AY)
were leaving he said to them, “Don’t quarrel on the way!”
(AZ)

25 So they went up out of Egypt and came to their father Jacob in the land of
(BA)
Canaan. 26 They told him, “Joseph is still alive! In fact, he is ruler of all Egypt.”

47 Joseph went and told Pharaoh, “My father and brothers, with their flocks and herds
and everything they own, have come from the land of Canaan and are now in
(A)
Goshen.” 2 He chose five of his brothers and presented them before Pharaoh.
(B) (C)
3 Pharaoh asked the brothers, “What is your occupation?”
(D)
“Your servants are shepherds, ” they replied to Pharaoh, “just as our fathers were.” 4
(E) (F)
They also said to him, “We have come to live here for a while, because the famine is
(G)
severe in Canaan and your servants’ flocks have no pasture. So now, please let your
(H) (I)
servants settle in Goshen.”
(J)
5 Pharaoh said to Joseph, “Your father and your brothers have come to you, 6 and the
land of Egypt is before you; settle your father and your brothers in the best part of the
(K)
land. Let them live in Goshen. And if you know of any among them with special
(L)
ability, put them in charge of my own livestock. ”
(M) (N)
7 Then Joseph brought his father Jacob in and presented him before Pharaoh. After
(O)
Jacob blessed Pharaoh, 8 Pharaoh asked him, “How old are you?”
[a] (P)
9 And Jacob said to Pharaoh, “The years of my pilgrimage are a hundred and thirty.
(Q)
My years have been few and difficult, and they do not equal the years of the pilgrimage
(R)
of my fathers. ” 10 Then Jacob blessed Pharaoh and went out from his presence.
(S) [b] (T)
11 So Joseph settled his father and his brothers in Egypt and gave them property in the
best part of the land, the district of Rameses, as Pharaoh directed. 12 Joseph also
(U) (V)
provided his father and his brothers and all his father’s household with food, according
to the number of their children.

Joseph and the Famine

13 There was no food, however, in the whole region because the famine was severe;
both Egypt and Canaan wasted away because of the famine. 14 Joseph collected all
(X)
the money that was to be found in Egypt and Canaan in payment for the grain they were
buying, and he brought it to Pharaoh’s palace. 15 When the money of the people of
(Y) (Z)
Egypt and Canaan was gone, all Egypt came to Joseph and said, “Give us food.
(AA) (AB)
Why should we die before your eyes? Our money is all gone.”
(AC)
16 “Then bring your livestock, ” said Joseph. “I will sell you food in exchange for your
(AD)
livestock, since your money is gone. ” 17 So they brought their livestock to Joseph,
(AE)
and he gave them food in exchange for their horses, their sheep and goats, their
(AF)
cattle and donkeys. And he brought them through that year with food in exchange for
(AG)
all their livestock.
18 When that year was over, they came to him the following year and said, “We cannot
hide from our lord the fact that since our money is gone and our livestock belongs to
(AH)
you, there is nothing left for our lord except our bodies and our land. 19 Why should
(AI)
we perish before your eyes —we and our land as well? Buy us and our land in
(AJ)
exchange for food, and we with our land will be in bondage to Pharaoh. Give us
(AK) (AL)
seed so that we may live and not die, and that the land may not become desolate.”
(AM)
20 So Joseph bought all the land in Egypt for Pharaoh. The Egyptians, one and all, sold
their fields, because the famine was too severe for them. The land became
(AN)
Pharaoh’s, 21 and Joseph reduced the people to servitude, from one end of Egypt to
[c](AO)
the other. 22 However, he did not buy the land of the priests, because they received a
(AP)
regular allotment from Pharaoh and had food enough from the allotment Pharaoh
(AQ)
gave them. That is why they did not sell their land.
23 Joseph said to the people, “Now that I have bought you and your land today for
Pharaoh, here is seed for you so you can plant the ground. 24 But when the crop
(AR) (AS)

comes in, give a fifth of it to Pharaoh. The other four-fifths you may keep as seed for
(AT)
the fields and as food for yourselves and your households and your children.”
25 “You have saved our lives,” they said. “May we find favor in the eyes of our lord; we
(AU)
will be in bondage to Pharaoh.”
(AV)
26 So Joseph established it as a law concerning land in Egypt—still in force today—that
a fifth of the produce belongs to Pharaoh. It was only the land of the priests that did
(AW)
not become Pharaoh’s.
(AX)

there and were fruitful and increased greatly in number.
(AZ) (BA)

(BB)
forty-seven. 29 When the time drew near for Israel to die, he called for his son
(BC) (BD) (BE)

(BF)
thigh and promise that you will show me kindness and faithfulness. Do not bury
(BG) (BH) (BI)
me in Egypt, 30 but when I rest with my fathers, carry me out of Egypt and bury me
(BJ)
where they are buried.”
(BK)
“I will do as you say,” he said.

(A)

The Death of Jacob
29 Then he gave them these instructions: “I am about to be gathered to my
(CA)
people. Bury me with my fathers in the cave in the field of Ephron the Hittite, 30
(CB) (CC) (CD)

(CE) (CF)

(CG) (CH) (CI)
and his wife Sarah were buried, there Isaac and his wife Rebekah were buried, and
(CJ) (CK)
there I buried Leah. 32 The field and the cave in it were bought from the Hittites. ”
(CL) [p](CM)
33 When Jacob had finished giving instructions to his sons, he drew his feet up into the
bed, breathed his last and was gathered to his people.

Genesis 50
50 Joseph threw himself on his father and wept over him and kissed him. 2 Then
(A)
Joseph directed the physicians in his service to embalm his father Israel. So the
physicians embalmed him, 3 taking a full forty days, for that was the time required for
(B)
embalming. And the Egyptians mourned for him seventy days.
(C)
4 When the days of mourning had passed, Joseph said to Pharaoh’s court, “If I have
(D) (E)
found favor in your eyes, speak to Pharaoh for me. Tell him, 5 ‘My father made me
(F)
swear an oath and said, “I am about to die; bury me in the tomb I dug for myself in
(G) (H) (I)
the land of Canaan.” Now let me go up and bury my father; then I will return.’”
(J) (K)
6 Pharaoh said, “Go up and bury your father, as he made you swear to do.”
7 So Joseph went up to bury his father. All Pharaoh’s officials accompanied him—the
(L)
dignitaries of his court and all the dignitaries of Egypt— 8 besides all the members of
(M)
Joseph’s household and his brothers and those belonging to his father’s household.
(N)
Only their children and their flocks and herds were left in Goshen. 9 Chariots and
(O) (P)
horsemen also went up with him. It was a very large company.
[a]

10 When they reached the threshing floor of Atad, near the Jordan, they lamented
(Q)
loudly and bitterly; and there Joseph observed a seven-day period of mourning for
(R) (S) (T)
his father. 11 When the Canaanites who lived there saw the mourning at the
(U) (V)
threshing floor of Atad, they said, “The Egyptians are holding a solemn ceremony of
mourning.” That is why that place near the Jordan is called Abel Mizraim.
(W) [b]
12 So Jacob’s sons did as he had commanded them: 13 They carried him to the land
(X)
of Canaan and buried him in the cave in the field of Machpelah, near Mamre, which
(Y) (Z)
Abraham had bought along with the field as a burial place from Ephron the Hittite.
(AA) (AB)
14 After burying his father, Joseph returned to Egypt, together with his brothers and all
the others who had gone with him to bury his father.
(AC)
Joseph Reassures His Brothers
15 When Joseph’s brothers saw that their father was dead, they said, “What if Joseph
holds a grudge against us and pays us back for all the wrongs we did to him?” 16
(AD) (AE)
So they sent word to Joseph, saying, “Your father left these instructions before he
(AF)
died: 17 ‘This is what you are to say to Joseph: I ask you to forgive your brothers the
sins and the wrongs they committed in treating you so badly.’ Now please forgive
(AG) (AH)
the sins of the servants of the God of your father. ” When their message came to him,
(AI)
Joseph wept.
(AJ)
18 His brothers then came and threw themselves down before him. “We are your
(AK)
slaves,” they said.
(AL)
19 But Joseph said to them, “Don’t be afraid. Am I in the place of God? 20 You
(AM)
intended to harm me, but God intended it for good to accomplish what is now
(AN) (AO) (AP)
being done, the saving of many lives. 21 So then, don’t be afraid. I will provide for you
(AQ)
and your children. ” And he reassured them and spoke kindly to them.
(AR) (AS)

The Death of Joseph
22 Joseph stayed in Egypt, along with all his father’s family. He lived a hundred and ten
years 23 and saw the third generation of Ephraim’s children. Also the
(AT) (AU) (AV) (AW)
children of Makir son of Manasseh were placed at birth on Joseph’s knees.
(AX) (AY) [c](AZ)
24 Then Joseph said to his brothers, “I am about to die. But God will surely come to
(BA)
your aid and take you up out of this land to the land he promised on oath to
(BB) (BC)
Abraham, Isaac and Jacob.” 25 And Joseph made the Israelites swear an
(BD) (BE) (BF)
oath and said, “God will surely come to your aid, and then you must carry my
(BG)
bones up from this place.”
(BH) (BI)
26 So Joseph died at the age of a hundred and ten. And after they embalmed
(BJ) (BK)
him, he was placed in a coffin in Egypt.
(BL)
